### B. Where I still disagree with Codex

**B1. Dropping the event ID is an API gap, not an ordering defect, for FIX-153 rows.** [PE]
- Replay re-sorts the SQL-ordered list with `Stream.sorted`, which is stable on ordered lists, so the SQL ID order survives on ties.
- Direct FIX-153 rows take `ordering_slot=1` under a unique key (`V106:5-7`; `MarketPriceEventService:62`).
- Protection rejects any observation older than an already-applied one (`LivePositionProtectionService:67`).
- So applied direct rows for a symbol rise strictly in time, in commit order.
- Refinement: ties are still possible for LEGACY rows and for collector-era rows (`COLLECTOR_KLINE_LIVE`, inserted with a NULL slot at `SharedMarketConsumer:489-492`).
- Windows that span the collector-to-direct switch can be out of order, because the out-of-order check ignores the collector source (`MarketPriceEventService:78`).
- Codex's point survives in a different form: the certificate's "evidence reference" (RPR-L-04) needs the ID, which is an unregistered API change.

**B2. "Complete price coverage" and "proof that observations could detect TP/SL crossings" test market fidelity, not parity.** [AI from PE]
- Production never saw crossings that fell between its observations. Requiring Replay to detect them would make Replay diverge from Production.
- For the live-protection path, the right test is that Replay has every observation Production applied. Section D (correction to B2) sets out where this argument stops.

### C. Concessions to Codex
- **FIX-11K and FIX-11Q do not change baseline decisions.** [PE] Neither observer calls a shared service or `replayScope`. Two smaller facts remain:
  - their database writes have no error handling (`OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver:86, 216`; `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver:81`), so an observer failure aborts the whole baseline run;
  - FIX-11Q reads the Production `wallet_settings` row (`:195`).

  The spec should state these facts instead of saying the observers "contaminate" parity runs.
- **My earlier "can never succeed" was too absolute.** I narrowed it last round. Finding E4 below gives that narrowed claim a stronger basis.

### D. Corrections to my previous round

| # | What I claimed | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| D5 | The shared-market path "does not update `market_price_event`" | **Wrong; retracted.** [PE] It inserts PENDING rows (`SharedMarketConsumer:488-493`), sets APPLIED or HISTORICAL_ONLY (`:502, :516`), and recovery also sets HISTORICAL_ONLY (`Fix150ProtectionRecovery:88`). What survives: in the FIX-150 parallel path (`:708-733`), the trading effects commit in one transaction and the status is copied in a later one (`:502`). Only FIX-153 direct rows guarantee that APPLIED means the effects committed in the same transaction (`MarketPriceEventService:82-98`). I also overstated that identifying the era would require reading retired tables: the `source` column identifies it. What that table cannot show is whether collector-era updates were atomic. |
| B2 | "A price Production never marked APPLIED is a price Production never acted on" | **True only for live protection.** [PE] An observation rejected at input leaves no row at all (`MarketPriceEventService:53-58`), and protection is skipped (`BinanceKlineService:108`). But the candle-close event still fires (`:110-111`), and the signal path then acts on the candle's close price (`PaperTradingService:170-195`; `PositionManagementService:73-76, 129-144`). Gaps should still not cause a refusal, but Replay must reproduce signal-path exits from candle-close prices, whatever the price-event status. |
| D2 | I listed the advisory stop-loss next to the routine profit-lock exit | **Narrowed.** [PE] The advisory stop-loss, the signal-path take-profit (which skips the continuation check, `:170-174`) and the signal-path stop-loss (`:182-185`) are normally pre-empted, because live protection has already handled the same closing price (`LivePositionProtectionService:100, 174`). They only act when that closing observation was not applied. **The label-free profit-lock exit is still routine.** `evaluate(signal)` reads no signal labels (`DynamicProfitLockService:46-51`; `ProfitLockPolicy:51`), and a live "protected hold" leaves the lock ACTIVE (`LivePositionProtectionService:229-230`). So in Production, any candle close at or below the lock exits (`PaperTradingService:176-180`), while Replay holds whenever the labels say hold. |
| D6 | Shared signal generation branches on Replay flags | **Precision fix.** [PE] The `historicalReplay` flag is also set by Production's own HISTORICAL_ONLY path (`AnalysisService:173`). Only the derivatives branch on `regressionReplay` (`:331`) is Replay-only. `MarketContextService:49-60` has its own separate branches. |
| D4 | Replay analyses unclosed (`closed=0`) candle rows | **Strengthened.** [PE] Replay never runs Production's candle data-quality check; its only caller is `CandleClosedAnalysisWorker:119`. Replay also skips the check that the snapshot belongs to the expected candle (`TechnicalIndicatorService:102-106`). So Replay creates signals for candles that Production blocked for data quality, dropped from its 1m queue (`CandleAnalysisDispatcher:74-79`), or recovered without a close token. |

D1 (Replay processes the candle-close signal before the closing price is protected) and D3 (the Replay context holds one opportunity and one latest price, so it cannot run several symbols) were both re-checked and are unchanged.

### E. New evidence this round (both agents missed it)

**E1. Missing order-book data has opposite effects in Production and Replay.** [PE]
- With no observations in the window, Production returns `INSUFFICIENT_DATA_HOLD` and blocks the entry (`OrderBookLiquidityService:190-199, 209-218`).
- With no stored rows, Replay returns `UNAVAILABLE` and leaves the entry permission unchanged (`:154-159`).
- The final decision combines that permission with AND (`FinalDecisionService:143-148`), so Replay can enter exactly where Production held.
- Snapshot storage is best-effort and silently drops rows when its queue is full or an insert fails, leaving no completeness marker (`OrderBookSnapshotService:44-66`).
- Production decides from an in-memory window, which can differ from the stored rows and is emptied on restart.
- So the spec's A5.6 test ("evidence exists") cannot prove completeness: a partly stored window looks valid.

**E2. The spec's parity goal conflicts with its own no-look-ahead rule.** [PE + AI]
- Production's HISTORICAL_ONLY path (`CandleClosedAnalysisWorker:160-165` Г+' `AnalysisService:166-175`) still calls the **live** derivatives service (`AnalysisService:331-335`). That service fetches current funding and open-interest data whatever timestamp it is given (`DerivativesPositioningService:52-60`). In the same analysis, `MarketContextService:56-58` uses the historical stub.
- Those signals are saved (`AnalysisService:174-175`), and later Production decisions read them as context:
  - live protection labels (`LivePositionProtectionService:95-97`);
  - entry/exit validation (`TradeExecutionValidationService:376-378`);
  - multi-timeframe confluence (`MultiTimeframeConfluenceService:109-111`).
- In periods when scheduled analysis was enabled, recovered signals were also executable (`ScheduledAnalysisService:203`; `AnalysisService:118-126`).
- [AI] Reproducing these signals exactly needs data from after the replay clock, which A6.4 and RPR-T-05 forbid. The spec does not address this.
- Live analysis also calls the derivatives service twice (`MarketContextService:59`; `AnalysisService:334`), so any capture under RPR-06 must record both results.

**E3. The ordering reversal (D1) can make Replay skip entries entirely.** [PE + AI]
- Replay applies the 15-second price-freshness rule to the last observation at or before the candle boundary (`ExecutionPriceAuthorityService:37-43`).
- Production applies it at the moment of processing, which is after the closing observation (`:46-48`; `PaperTradingService:342-343`).
- When the last in-candle update is more than 15 s before the boundary, Replay skips an entry or add (`ShadowProductionReplayService:310-312, 345-347`) that Production executed.
- [AI] This is plausible for thinly traded symbols. An earlier investigation (non-authoritative memory, not re-checked this round) recorded gaps of 2Г?"59 s between applied SHIB price events.

**E4. Historical settings are not versioned, so A5.8 cannot be met for any past window.** [PE]
- `wallet_settings` is a single, mutable row (`V28:13`), and I found no settings-history table in the migrations.
- Replay also re-reads the current settings on every price event (`ShadowProductionReplayService:726, 757, 777, 1012`), so a single run can mix settings if they are edited mid-run.
- Derivatives make it worse: when enabled, every decision passes through the derivatives stage (`FinalDecisionService:135-141`) and its confidence adjustment (`MarketContextService:98`), so A5.7 refuses every request in that period.
- Result: parity mode cannot succeed for any window before forward capture exists, unless some configuration history exists outside the database.

**External research [ER]:** Binance Developers, "WebSocket Streams" (Spot), https://developers.binance.com/docs/binance-spot-api-docs/web-socket-streams.
- Date: none shown; accessed 2026-10-10.
- Findings: each kline message carries `E` (event time), `T` (close time) and `x` (closed flag). Updates arrive every 2000 ms for intervals other than 1s. A connection is valid for at most 24 hours.
- Limitations: the page does not say how far the closing (`x=true`) message's `E` falls after `T`; its sample shows an open candle.
- Relevance: how often D1 and E3 occur must be measured from project data. This says nothing about trading performance.

## OBJECTIONS (material, unresolved)
1. **Contract granularity.** There is no class/method registry, no migration IDs, no configuration keys and no per-artifact IDs (AGENTS.md:337; template `:40-144`). Both agents raised this.
2. **Production impact is misstated.** The spec says "Trading behaviour: NONE" and that Production needs no rollback, yet these items change Production classes:
   - RPR-03 / A5.1: `TradeExecutionValidationService`;
   - RPR-03a: `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which is also on Production's near-TP path;
   - A6.2: the `AnalysisService` and `MarketContextService` branches;
   - RPR-06 capture: `DerivativesPositioningService` and `OrderBookSnapshotService`.

   Each needs a registry entry, latency and transaction impact, regression tests and a code rollback.
3. **Unregistered divergences.** RPR-T-03 and RPR-T-04 cannot pass until the spec registers:
   - D1 ordering, the price-time difference and E3;
   - the label-free profit-lock exit, and the signal-path exits that act when a closing observation was not applied;
   - profit-lock suppression of SELLs and adds;
   - same-boundary timeframe visibility;
   - candles Production blocked for data quality, ran as HISTORICAL_ONLY, dropped from the queue, or recovered without a token;
   - E1;
   - daily-budget timing (`WalletAutoExecutionService:102, 689-704`).
4. **Evidence qualification is defined the wrong way.** "Coverage" should be replaced with:
   - for live protection: every applied observation present, no PENDING rows, era taken from `source`, and a tie rule for NULL-slot rows;
   - candle provenance plus Production's per-candle outcome as the decision oracle (`candle_analysis_outcome`, `trade_signal.signal_mode`, `signal_processing_work.status`);
   - configuration and code version as of each decision.

   RPR-T-02 must not refuse because of deliberately excluded rows (AGENTS.md:187-189).
5. **Pre-write refusal contradiction.** A5 requires refusal before anything is written, but A5.5's price range is only known after execution.
6. **No comparison oracle.** Decision-level parity and full position-lifecycle parity need to be defined and tested separately.
7. **New: parity conflicts with no-look-ahead (E2).** The spec must choose one approach and state it:
   - refuse windows whose decisions depend on signals built with later data; or
   - use Production's stored signals as historical context instead of regenerating them.
8. **Default mode is unreachable before capture exists (E4 plus derivatives).** Either register forward capture as a Production change, or do not make parity the default yet.
9. **Multi-symbol design gap (D3).** The spec also gives no rule for ordering competing claims on global limits across symbols.
10. **New: order-book completeness (E1).** A5.6 needs a completeness marker or an explicit refusal rule, and the opposite empty-window behaviour must be registered.
11. **Base revision and governance contradiction.** Three sources disagree, and per AGENTS.md this must be reported and investigated:
    - V109 says V107 (FIX-154) "was already applied on the trader database";
    - AGENTS.md:204 calls FIX-154 not yet implemented;
    - `TradeExecutionValidationService` already contains FIX-154 code.

    The spec must state which revision is the parity target.
12. **Removal scope.** Each removal item needs a fixed decision for every API, UI, table and archive path, including the FIX-11K endpoints at `RegressionTestController:76-77, 120-123`.

**Non-material:**
- Codex's `:338` citation (see A).
- The Production near-TP check uses the wall clock (`LivePositionProtectionService:256`), while Replay uses the event time (`ShadowProductionReplayService:521`).
- Price events after the last signal are never processed by Replay (`:126-128`).

## UNKNOWNS
1. **How often D1 and E3 occur.** This depends on how far the closing observation lands after the candle boundary. A bounded read-only query would answer it: join closed 1m `candle` rows to APPLIED `market_price_event` rows with `price = close_price` and `observed_at` within a few seconds of `open_time + 60s`, per symbol and day.
2. **Deployed configuration and code.** The deployed derivatives, order-book, sentiment and `trading.fix122.enabled` values are unknown, as is whether FIX-153 and FIX-154 are deployed.
3. **Historical settings.** I don't know whether any record of past `wallet_settings` values exists outside the database.
4. **LEGACY rows (before V89).** Whether each was recorded before or after protection, and whether Production acted on it, is unknown.
5. **Frequency of silent data loss.** How often order-book rows were dropped is visible only in WARN logs (`OrderBookSnapshotService:58, 63`). How often closing observations were rejected at input is visible only in `PRICE_NOT_CANONICAL` logs.
6. **No execution.** Nothing was compiled or run.

## VERDICT
Codex's Round-1 evidence holds; I found only a minor citation slip.
- I retracted part of my own D5 and narrowed B2, D2 and D6.
- This round adds four code-proven problems: E1 (order-book behaviour inverted), E2 (parity versus no-look-ahead), E3 (Replay skipping entries) and E4 (settings not versioned).
- Each of them alone prevents RPR-0.4 from supporting a parity claim.
- This review does not authorise any change.

Nothing was written: plan mode is active and this session has no write tool.

VERDICT: REJECTED

- [Binance Spot Г?" WebSocket Streams (legacy docs)](https://developers.binance.com/legacy-docs/binance-spot-api-docs/web-socket-streams)

  28: ## 3. Additional source findings to preserve in the next scope
  29: 
  30: The independent review identified source-backed gaps that must become registry
  31: items rather than prose aspirations:
  32: 
  33: - Replay currently can analyse a `closed=0` candle and use an earlier candle
  34:   snapshot. A parity run needs durable candle identity/finality checks.
  35: - The live close path and Replay do not necessarily see the same 1m decision
  36:   at a candle boundary. Event lineage, not a loose timestamp sort, must define
  37:   the replay ordering rule.
  38: - Production can subsequently close an active profit lock without rechecking
  39:   labels, while current Replay may retain the position. It also has an advisory
  40:   signal-path stop-loss that Replay does not invoke.
  41: - Historical derivatives/order-book/sentiment evidence may be unavailable or
  42:   have only event time rather than record/availability time. Unknown evidence
  43:   must produce a diagnostic refusal for parity, not a no-look-ahead claim.
  44: - A Replay account must never read Production `wallet_*` state. The forbidden
  45:   scope must name the Production tables precisely while allowing Replay-owned
  46:   test/result tables where approved.
  47: - A manual replay run executes in the same JVM and database pool as the trader.
  48:   Its resource limits and cancellation behaviour must protect Production
  49:   freshness and must be tested.
  50:

  52:

  56: - Clear `RegimeStateService` replay state deterministically after every run.
  57: - State whether any new Replay evidence pre-write extends or replaces the
  58:   current pending-evidence guard.
  59: - Document that current paper trades use zero fees, so parity is not a claim
  60:   about exchange-net profitability.
  61: - Test cancellation/worker completion, two-request admission behaviour,
  62:   curated and random/incident windows, look-ahead, and refusal-rate reporting.

 165:     public OrderBookLiquidityResult evaluate(
 166:             String symbol,
 167:             String interval,
 168:             SignalDecision currentDecision,
 169:             boolean entryAllowed,
 170:             BigDecimal currentPrice,
 171:             BigDecimal stopLoss,
 172:             BigDecimal takeProfit,
 173:             Instant evaluatedAt
 174:     ) {
 175:         IntervalPolicy policy = properties.policyFor(interval == null ? "5m" : interval);
 176:         Instant snapshotTime = evaluatedAt == null ? Instant.now() : evaluatedAt;
 177:         if (!properties.enabled()) {
 178:             return result(LiquidityContextStatus.DISABLED, currentDecision, currentDecision,
 179:                     entryAllowed, null, null, null, null, null, null, null, null,
 180:                     false, false, 0, "Order-book liquidity analysis is disabled.", snapshotTime,
 181:                     policy, 0L);
 182:         }
 183: 
 184:         String normalizedSymbol = normalize(symbol);
 185:         Deque<OrderBookSnapshot> deque = historyBySymbol.get(normalizedSymbol);
 186:         if (deque == null || deque.isEmpty()) {
 187:             collectSafely(normalizedSymbol);
 188:             deque = historyBySymbol.get(normalizedSymbol);
 189:         }
 190:         if (deque == null || deque.isEmpty()) {
 191:             // FIX-091 / Fix 2: in LIVE analysis, zero observations are not proof that liquidity is safe.
 192:             // Hold a fresh entry until the configured sample minimum is reached. Historical replay uses
 193:             // evaluateHistorical(...) and deliberately keeps its separate UNAVAILABLE limitation.
 194:             return result(LiquidityContextStatus.INSUFFICIENT_DATA_HOLD, currentDecision, currentDecision,
 195:                     false, null, null, null, null, null, null, null, null,
 196:                     false, false, 0, "Live order-book sampling is not ready (0/" + policy.minimumObservations()
 197:                             + "). Fresh entry is held until enough observations are collected.", snapshotTime,
 198:                     policy, 0L);
 199:         }
 200: 
 201:         List<OrderBookSnapshot> snapshots;
 202:         synchronized (deque) {
 203:             Instant windowStart = snapshotTime.minusSeconds(policy.windowSeconds());
 204:             snapshots = deque.stream()
 205:                     .filter(snapshot -> !snapshot.capturedAt().isAfter(snapshotTime))
 206:                     .filter(snapshot -> !snapshot.capturedAt().isBefore(windowStart))
 207:                     .toList();
 208:         }
 209:         if (snapshots.isEmpty()) {
 210:             // FIX-091 / Fix 2: an enabled LIVE order-book service with no observation in the active
 211:             // window is an insufficient-sampling condition, not a neutral PASS.
 212:             return result(LiquidityContextStatus.INSUFFICIENT_DATA_HOLD, currentDecision, currentDecision,
 213:                     false, null, null, null, null, null, null, null, null,
 214:                     false, false, 0,
 215:                     "No live order-book observation exists inside the " + policy.windowSeconds()
 216:                             + " second window for interval " + interval + "; fresh entry is held until "
 217:                             + policy.minimumObservations() + " observations are available.",
 218:                     snapshotTime, policy, 0L);
 219:         }

The response correctly keeps RPR-0.4 rejected and carries several core objections forward. I’ve confirmed two material omissions in source already: Replay writes generated-signal rows before the current evidence guard, and its historical order-book fallback can permit an entry where live Production holds it.

   1: package com.crypto.regression.service;
   2: 
   3: import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
   4: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
   5: 
   6: import java.util.ArrayList;
   7: import java.util.List;
   8: import java.util.Objects;
   9: 
  10: /**
  11:  * FIX-091 / Fix 6: field-level pre-wallet parity comparator.
  12:  * It deliberately compares trading-intelligence state only; wallet execution is outside scope.
  13:  */
  14: @Service
  15: public class ReplayParityValidator {
  16: 
  17:     public Result compare(TradeSignal production, TradeSignal replay) {
  18:         List<String> mismatches = new ArrayList<>();
  19:         if (production == null || replay == null) {
  20:             mismatches.add("Production and Replay signals must both exist");
  21:             return new Result(false, mismatches);
  22:         }
  23: 
  24:         same(mismatches, "detectedRegime", production.getDetectedRegime(), replay.getDetectedRegime());
  25:         same(mismatches, "candidateRegime", production.getCandidateRegime(), replay.getCandidateRegime());
  26:         same(mismatches, "confirmedRegime", production.getConfirmedRegime(), replay.getConfirmedRegime());
  27:         same(mismatches, "selectedStrategy", production.getSelectedStrategy(), replay.getSelectedStrategy());
  28:         same(mismatches, "originalDecision", production.getOriginalDecision(), replay.getOriginalDecision());
  29:         same(mismatches, "finalDecision", production.getDecision(), replay.getDecision());
  30:         same(mismatches, "rawConfidence", production.getRawConfidenceScore(), replay.getRawConfidenceScore());
  31:         same(mismatches, "effectiveConfidence", production.getEffectiveConfidenceScore(), replay.getEffectiveConfidenceScore());
  32:         same(mismatches, "primaryBlockingStage", production.getPrimaryBlockingStage(), replay.getPrimaryBlockingStage());
  33:         same(mismatches, "btcContextStatus", production.getBtcContextStatus(), replay.getBtcContextStatus());
  34:         same(mismatches, "liquidityStatus", production.getLiquidityStatus(), replay.getLiquidityStatus());
  35:         same(mismatches, "entryAuthority", production.getEntryAuthority(), replay.getEntryAuthority());
  36:         same(mismatches, "entryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent",
  37:                 production.getEntryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent(), replay.getEntryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent());
  38:         return new Result(mismatches.isEmpty(), List.copyOf(mismatches));
  39:     }
  40: 
  41:     private void same(List<String> mismatches, String field, Object expected, Object actual) {
  42:         if (!Objects.equals(expected, actual)) {
  43:             mismatches.add(field + ": Production=" + expected + ", Replay=" + actual);
  44:         }
  45:     }
  46: 
  47:     public record Result(boolean matches, List<String> mismatches) {}
  48: }

--- src/main/java/com/crypto/wallet/service/BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy.java:20-65
  20:  * This class deliberately checks only the agreed minimum executable notional boundary.
  21:  * It does not add a new Near-TP-specific step-size/precision model.
  22:  */
  23: @Component
  24: @RequiredArgsConstructor
  25: @Slf4j
  26: public class BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy {
  27:     private static final MathContext MC = MathContext.DECIMAL64;
  28: 
  29:     private final BinanceMarketDataClient binanceMarketDataClient;
  30:     public Evaluation evaluate(String symbol, BigDecimal quantity, BigDecimal price) {
  31:         if (symbol == null || symbol.isBlank() || quantity == null || quantity.signum() <= 0
  32:                 || price == null || price.signum() <= 0) {
  33:             return new Evaluation(false, "INVALID_ORDER_INPUT", BigDecimal.ZERO, null);
  34:         }
  35:         String pair = symbol.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);
  36:         BigDecimal notional = quantity.multiply(price, MC);
  37:         BigDecimal minimum;
  38:         try {
  39:             minimum = binanceMarketDataClient.getMinimumExecutableNotional(pair);
  40:         } catch (RuntimeException ex) {
  41:             // Fail safe. Missing exchange constraints must never be converted into permission
  42:             // to create an order that real Binance might reject.
  43:             log.warn("FIX-11T Binance minimum executable amount unavailable: symbol={}, requestedNotional={}, error={}",
  44:                     pair, notional, ex.getMessage());
  45:             return new Evaluation(false, "BINANCE_MINIMUM_UNAVAILABLE", notional, null);
  46:         }
  47:         if (notional.compareTo(minimum) < 0) {
  48:             return new Evaluation(false, "BELOW_BINANCE_MINIMUM", notional, minimum);
  49:         }
  50:         return new Evaluation(true, "EXECUTABLE", notional, minimum);
  51:     }
  52: 
  53:     public record Evaluation(boolean executable, String code, BigDecimal requestedNotional, BigDecimal minimumNotional) {
  54:     }
  55: }

--- src/main/java/com/crypto/client/binance/BinanceMarketDataClient.java:150-210
 150:     }
 151: 
 152: 
 153:     /**
 154:      * FIX-11T: read the symbol's current Binance minimum executable notional directly
 155:      * from exchangeInfo.  Near-TP uses this only as a minimum-order guard; it does not
 156:      * introduce a separate quantity-rounding model. NOTIONAL is preferred when present,
 157:      * with MIN_NOTIONAL retained for symbols that still expose the older filter.
 158:      */
 159:     public BigDecimal getMinimumExecutableNotional(String symbol) {
 160:         if (symbol == null || symbol.isBlank()) {
 161:             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Symbol is required");
 162:         }
 163: 
 164:         Map<String, Object> response = request(() -> restClient.get()
 165:             .uri(uriBuilder -> uriBuilder
 166:                 .path("/api/v3/exchangeInfo")
 167:                 .queryParam("symbol", symbol.toUpperCase())
 168:                 .build())
 169:             .retrieve()
 170:             .body(new ParameterizedTypeReference<>() {
 171:             }));
 172: 
 173:         if (response == null) {
 174:             throw new IllegalStateException("Empty Binance exchangeInfo response for " + symbol);
 175:         }
 176:         Object symbolsValue = response.get("symbols");
 177:         if (!(symbolsValue instanceof List<?> symbols) || symbols.isEmpty() || !(symbols.get(0) instanceof Map<?, ?> symbolInfo)) {
 178:             throw new IllegalStateException("Binance exchangeInfo did not contain symbol rules for " + symbol);
 179:         }
 180:         Object filtersValue = symbolInfo.get("filters");
 181:         if (!(filtersValue instanceof List<?> filters)) {
 182:             throw new IllegalStateException("Binance exchangeInfo did not contain filters for " + symbol);
 183:         }
 184: 
 185:         BigDecimal minNotional = null;
 186:         for (Object filterValue : filters) {
 187:             if (!(filterValue instanceof Map<?, ?> filter)) continue;
 188:             String filterType = String.valueOf(filter.get("filterType"));
 189:             if (!"NOTIONAL".equals(filterType) && !"MIN_NOTIONAL".equals(filterType)) continue;
 190:             Object raw = filter.get("minNotional");
 191:             if (raw == null) continue;
 192:             BigDecimal candidate = new BigDecimal(String.valueOf(raw));
 193:             if (candidate.signum() <= 0) continue;
 194:             if ("NOTIONAL".equals(filterType)) return candidate;
 195:             minNotional = candidate;
 196:         }
 197:         if (minNotional != null) return minNotional;
 198:         throw new IllegalStateException("Binance exchangeInfo did not expose a minimum notional for " + symbol);
 199:     }
 200: 
 201:     private BinanceKline mapKline(List<Object> row) {
 202:         if (row == null || row.size() < 11) {
 203:             throw new IllegalArgumentException(
 204:                 "Invalid Binance kline response"
 205:             );
 206:         }
 207: 
 208:         return new BinanceKline(
 209:             Instant.ofEpochMilli(toLong(row.get(0))),
 210:             toDecimal(row.get(1)),

 543:     private void enforceDailyLossLimit() {
 544:         Instant startOfDay = LocalDate.now(ZoneOffset.UTC).atStartOfDay().toInstant(ZoneOffset.UTC);
 545:         BigDecimal pnl = positionRepository.sumRealizedPnlSince(startOfDay);
 546:         BigDecimal maximumLoss = properties.paperAccountBalance()
 547:                 .multiply(properties.maxDailyLossPercent(), MC)
 548:                 .divide(BigDecimal.valueOf(100), MC)
 549:                 .negate();
 550:

 552:             throw new IllegalStateException("Daily loss limit reached");
 553:         }

  31:     public boolean eligible(SignalProcessingStore.Work work, Instant now) {
  32:         var candles=candleReader(jdbc).query("""
  33:                 SELECT open_time,close_time FROM candle WHERE symbol=? AND interval_code=?
  34:                 AND closed=1 AND close_time<=? ORDER BY open_time DESC LIMIT 1

20: | CR-03 | Current Replay misses production orchestration, not only wallet state. | Register the close-message/price-event lineage, label-free profit-lock exit, advisory stop-loss, SELL/add suppression during an active profit lock, progressive-add gate, analysis/fill clocks, historical-only/skipped candles, same-boundary higher-timeframe visibility and daily-budget timing. Each must be either implemented for parity, classified as an allowed divergence, or cause a parity refusal. |
21: | CR-04 | "Parity" has no usable oracle after the virtual account diverges from Production. | Define separate outcomes: (a) **decision parity**, compared with stored as-of Production decision evidence; (b) **lifecycle parity**, only for an authorised matching virtual starting state; and (c) **simulation**, useful output that must never claim parity. Classify scheduling/concurrency differences explicitly rather than silently accepting them. |
22: | CR-05 | Evidence qualification is incomplete and incorrectly treats deliberate exclusions as failures. | Define an era boundary and eligible evidence set. A parity run must use the same accepted/applied price observations Production could use; `NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` remain deliberate exclusions, not missing evidence. Require as-of candle identity/provenance, signal mode/outcome, configuration/version, sentiment availability time and applicable order-book/derivative evidence. A gap definition belongs in simulation diagnostics; parity refuses only when required accepted evidence is absent. |
23: | CR-06 | Default `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` is not currently reachable for evidence eras with derivatives/configuration history missing. | Do not make an impossible mode the default. The replacement must either (1) register forward capture of the missing evidence and make parity available only from that capture date, or (2) require the caller to choose `SIMULATION` explicitly and reject a parity request with exact reasons. |
24: | CR-07 | The base revision, migration sequencing and deployment facts conflict. | Freeze an exact Git commit/diff identity before design approval. Reconcile the observed migration/code state with AGENTS.md and actual Flyway history using read-only evidence. The replacement spec must state the base commit, migration numbers reserved, merge order, target database compatibility and the outcome if an expected table/migration is absent. |
25: | CR-08 | Multi-symbol/global-budget ordering is not designed. | Either restrict the first parity scope to one virtual account/symbol with an explicit refusal for multi-symbol runs, or register deterministic cross-symbol scheduling and global-limit claim ordering in `ExecutionReplayScope` with tests. |
26: | CR-09 | Replay-only removals are product decisions, not automatic parity repairs. | Do not remove FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, the pre-FIX122 selector, or `EXPERIMENTAL` mode in the parity change unless their callers, persisted data and user-facing workflows are inventoried and the user approves their retirement. Until then, isolate them from baseline parity and make their outcome clearly experimental. |

## FINDINGS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The response correctly preserves the rejection and does not pretend to amend or approve RPR-0.4. Its CR-01 through CR-09 are valid requirements for a replacement specification, particularly registry granularity, Production-impact disclosure, event-orchestration parity, oracle definition, evidence eras, baseline freeze, scope, and retirement decisions. [Response](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\agent-docs\RPR-0.4_REVIEW_RESPONSE.md:9)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The response is not a complete response to the prior objections. It omits the pre-write eligibility contradiction: current Replay persists generated signals before its price-evidence guard runs. A replacement must choose pre-qualified whole-window evidence or a fully in-memory preflight; it cannot certify a later refusal as “before writes.” [Worker](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\regression\service\RegressionTestWorker.java:174) [writes](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\regression\service\RegressionTestWorker.java:636) [guard](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\regression\service\RegressionTestWorker.java:195)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** CR-05 correctly keeps `NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` outside the accepted Replay price stream. However, it does not require a complete ordering/evidence model: `find()` retains only `LEGACY` and `APPLIED` rows and drops the event ID, while Replay subsequently sorts only by observed time. A parity certificate therefore cannot identify or deterministically order equal-time legacy/collector rows. [Service](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\market\service\MarketPriceEventService.java:118) [record shape](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\market\service\MarketPriceEventService.java:173)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** CR-05 also misses a material live-versus-Replay order-book inversion. With no live observations, Production attempts collection then returns `INSUFFICIENT_DATA_HOLD` and blocks entry; historical Replay returns `UNAVAILABLE` while retaining the incoming entry permission. Presence of some stored snapshots does not establish that the in-memory live decision window was complete. [Production path](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\OrderBookLiquidityService.java:184) [Replay path](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\OrderBookLiquidityService.java:154)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The response properly identifies unavailable derivatives/configuration history as a default-mode problem, but does not resolve the no-look-ahead conflict. Recovery analysis runs with `regressionReplay=false`, so it calls the live derivatives evaluator even when producing a `HISTORICAL_ONLY` signal. That evaluator retrieves current Binance data. The next specification must explicitly choose persisted Production-context injection—field sufficiency proven—or refusal; recomputation cannot claim both exact parity and no look-ahead. [Recovery](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\AnalysisService.java:167) [branch](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\AnalysisService.java:331) [HTTP evaluator](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\DerivativesPositioningService.java:52)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The wallet isolation wording remains ambiguous. A replay business decision must not use Production wallet/position/settings rows, but a decision/lifecycle oracle may need explicit read-only comparison access. Current chart output already reads `wallet_trade`. The next specification must separate a no-decision-input rule from an enumerated, non-locking, post-run oracle-read allowance—or prohibit that oracle and narrow the claim. [Response](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\agent-docs\RPR-0.4_REVIEW_RESPONSE.md:44) [current read](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\regression\service\RegressionTestService.java:549)

## EVIDENCE

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** Production has material orchestration absent from the response’s required list: signal-path TP, stop-loss, rebase floor, active-lock SELL suppression, progressive-add gating, global open-position cap, and an exception-throwing daily-loss gate. The next specification must reproduce, classify, or refuse each path—not merely test generic lifecycle equality. [Signal path](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\PaperTradingService.java:170) [adds](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\PaperTradingService.java:215) [limits](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\PaperTradingService.java:322) [daily loss](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\service\PaperTradingService.java:543)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** Production also expires stale work after locks and records `EXPIRED`; Replay has no equivalent execution-admission model. The next revision must treat execution freshness, lock/retry outcomes, and their clock authority as parity dimensions or refusal conditions. [Coordinator](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\execution\processing\SignalProcessingCoordinator.java:93) [freshness](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\execution\processing\SignalProcessingFreshness.java:31)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The current Replay implementation remains disqualified from the stated no-live-HTTP requirement: its minimum-notional policy calls Binance `exchangeInfo`. The response recognizes the broad issue but the replacement must carry a complete deny-by-default outbound-I/O inventory and an automated zero-outbound-call test. [Replay use](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\regression\service\ShadowProductionReplayService.java:534) [HTTP call](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\client\binance\BinanceMarketDataClient.java:159)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The baseline is not frozen. `HEAD` is `0cb6a9a6cc64ba8ac9d33797bcc30af534fd46e3`, while the working tree contains substantial tracked and untracked changes, including migrations and current local-candle/FIX-154 code. CR-07 is therefore necessary but must freeze the complete tree/diff identity, not just a commit.

## OBJECTIONS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** Do not approve a next specification unless it includes these registered requirements:

  1. A frozen full-tree baseline, target deployment/version/configuration fingerprint, Flyway compatibility policy, and explicit resolution of code-versus-guidance contradictions.
  2. Pre-write parity eligibility: exact whole-window qualification before generated-signal, shadow-wallet, or certificate writes.
  3. A precise oracle registry: decision, execution, protection, exit, and terminal-work outcomes; durable record, identity, as-of/availability time, equality rule, and absent-evidence result.
  4. A strict read boundary: Production wallet/position/settings data never influences replay decisions; any post-run oracle reads are enumerated, read-only, non-locking, and isolated.
  5. A context matrix for derivatives, order book, sentiment, fundamentals, BTC, and higher timeframes: recompute, inject persisted result, or refuse. Require field-sufficiency proof and no future-data use.
  6. A source-era/event-order contract retaining immutable price-event IDs; explicit tie handling; closing-message ordering; trailing price events; candle admission/outcome/queue-drop handling.
  7. Full orchestration treatment for signal exits, protection, locks, fills, expiry, retries, daily clocks, global limits, and all shared-policy transaction/latency effects.
  8. An order-book completeness policy addressing the live `HOLD` versus Replay `UNAVAILABLE` inversion; incomplete historical windows must refuse parity.
  9. First-release scope fixed as single-symbol with multi-symbol refusal, unless deterministic global scheduling and account-state semantics are completely specified.
  10. A complete no-live-I/O test, pre-write-refusal test, no-look-ahead tests, Production regression suite for every shared class, disposable-MySQL migration proof, cancellation/resource-isolation tests, and random/incident-window refusal and mismatch reporting.
  11. A user decision table for replay-only observers and legacy modes; baseline isolation is required now, deletion remains a separate approved product decision.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The response’s three outcome classes are directionally right, but “lifecycle parity” cannot be asserted merely because a virtual starting state is authorised. It must also prove the historical account constraints were non-binding or reproduce them from immutable, sufficient evidence. Otherwise the permitted conclusion is decision parity, simulation, or refusal.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `ReplayParityValidator` is not a lifecycle oracle: it compares signal-level fields only, and no production code invokes it. Reusing it requires a specified call site and expanded equality contract; otherwise it is not evidence of parity. [Validator](C:\workspace\crypto-ai-trader-mysql\src\main\java\com\crypto\regression\service\ReplayParityValidator.java:11)

## UNKNOWNS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** No database, Production system, deployment record, or runtime log was accessed. Therefore applied migration history, historical configuration, evidence-era dates, and deployed-code identity remain unverified.

- **AGENT INFERENCE:** Older windows are likely ineligible for full lifecycle parity unless durable historical account/configuration/context evidence is produced. This is not yet proven for any specific window.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** No build or test was run; this review verifies current source and documents only.
